## Shared findings from the last pass, and their disposition

Last pass (repair 4, 43 rows): codex + grok DO-NOT-SHIP; the Opus 5.5 seat MEASURED 15 of 18 mutants at 43/43, in four
classes -- the helper NARROWED (`mode & 0x17` admits 0x18-0x1a), the msr decline's width varying by label, a restore
decline FORCING a bit or OR-ing old bits in, T while latched -- plus two already disclosed.  agy voted SHIP; minimax,
glm, deepseek and kimi-k3 SHIP-WITH-CHANGES.  ALL ADOPTED.  Owner ruling 2026-10-08: **repair 5 GENERATES the
restore/MSR coverage** (G1-G4 below) instead of adding rows by hand.

## Measured on repair 5 (`regress/artifacts/armmsrmodekill/killtable5_20261008/`)

Product code UNCHANGED (fix5.diff byte-identical to fix4.diff).  fixed 47/47 (43 sessions); HEAD 18/47, 29 named reds.
Two tables in 8 lanes, every lane 47/47 before and after: **110 runs, 104 distinct mutants (the earlier set plus Opus's
18), 103 red, ONE at 47/47: `M_msr_argmask`** -- the msr decline ALSO writes `ic->arg[1] &= ~ARM_FLAG_MODE` (the cached
translation).

**And a records error in repair 5's probe, found while reading that escape, corrected before this pass (comment-only,
AST-identical to the measured probe):** its NOT CLAIMED said the grid's re-executions of `msr cpsr_fsxc,r1` "pin the
register form".  They cannot.  Every re-execution in the probe follows a debugger INTERACTION (to set r1, or to dump),
and `debugger()` clears every dyntrans translation when it interacts -- `src/debugger/debugger.c`, the loop under
"Clear all dyntrans translations" -- returning BEFORE that clear only while a `step N` count is still running.  So each
execution is re-translated and an `ic->arg[]` write never reaches the next one.  A guest loop re-executing ONE
translated MSR inside a single `step N`, changing the register between passes, would pin it; NOT built.

## Gate 14 wiring (NEW in this round -- review it): the section appended to `regress/gate_arm.sh`

```sh

#  #562 armmsrmodekill.  A privileged guest MSR naming a mode this model does not bank (MSR CPSR_c,#0x14 =
#  0xE321F014), or an SPSR restore (MOVS pc,lr; LDM {..pc}^) whose SPSR names one -- including an SPSR never written,
#  which is zero -- reached arm_load_register_bank()'s default: and ended the emulator.  msr_imm and both restores now
#  decline the MODE FIELD only, announced once per site per CPU, and Save/Load's default: arms are latched backstops.
#  The probe's CONTRACT, its generated grid and its NOT CLAIMED list are in its header; the kill tables and the
#  review chain's measurements are in regress/artifacts/armmsrmodekill/.  Rows do not end in their status token
#  (a note follows it), so failed rows are read from ARMMSRMODE_REDS, never grepped.
MSRLOG=$LOGDIR/gate_arm_msrmode.log
mrc=0
python3 -u arm_msrmode_probe.py "$PMAX" gate > "$MSRLOG" 2>&1 || mrc=$?

#  PARSE BEFORE SCORE: exactly one valid record of each kind.
if [ "$(grep -c '^ARMMSRMODE_RESULT=' "$MSRLOG")" != "1" ] ||
   ! grep -Eq '^ARMMSRMODE_RESULT=(0|[1-9][0-9]*)/(0|[1-9][0-9]*)$' "$MSRLOG" ||
   [ "$(grep -c '^ARMMSRMODE_CONTROL=' "$MSRLOG")" != "1" ] ||
   ! grep -Eq '^ARMMSRMODE_CONTROL=(OK|DEAD)$' "$MSRLOG" ||
   [ "$(grep -c '^ARMMSRMODE_REDS=' "$MSRLOG")" != "1" ] ||
   [ "$(grep -cx 'ARMMSRMODE_DONE' "$MSRLOG")" != "1" ]; then
    note "msrmode probe produced no unique, valid completion record; last lines follow"
    tail -5 "$MSRLOG" | sed 's/^/       /'
    check "msrmode probe output parses" "no" "yes"
else
    check "msrmode probe output parses" "yes" "yes"
    grep -E ' want=' "$MSRLOG" | grep -E ' FAIL ' | sed 's/^/       /'
    check "msrmode controls" "$(sed -n 's/^ARMMSRMODE_CONTROL=//p' "$MSRLOG")" "OK"
    check "msrmode probe exit status" "$mrc" "0"
    mres=$(sed -n 's/^ARMMSRMODE_RESULT=//p' "$MSRLOG")
    #  Pinned by hand to a LITERAL, UNQUOTED, under the ONE-TOKEN stem arm_msrmode (check_score_claims.py's PIN regex
    #  takes one whitespace-free token before `rows run'; the stem also puts the probe's docstring under that scan).
    #  MEASURED: <OWED the committed probe's base leg, named, run BEFORE this pin was written>.
    check "arm_msrmode rows run"     "${mres#*/}" 47
    check "arm_msrmode rows correct" "${mres%/*}" "47"
    check "arm_msrmode rows emitted" "$(grep -cE '^  [^ ].* got=.* want=' "$MSRLOG")" "47"
    check "arm_msrmode reds"         "$(sed -n 's/^ARMMSRMODE_REDS=//p' "$MSRLOG")" "none"
fi

```

## Probe header: the generated grid, CONTRACT, NOT CLAIMED, tally (verbatim, as corrected)

```
THE GENERATED GRID (repair 5; owner ruling 2026-10-08).  The restore/MSR coverage is GENERATED
from tables (grid_obs(): labels, MODELLED, UNMODELLED, complementary value pairs), not written row
by row: `grid msr', `grid dpi', `grid pop' run every generated observation and compare the WHOLE
CPSR word; `grid cov' re-derives the coverage table from the same generator and reds on any gap.
  G1 every (path, outcome, bit 0..31, polarity) cell has an observation whose result bit must
     equal the SPSR / operand value while the OLD CPSR's bit differs (complementary pairs:
     OLD 0 / NEW all-ones-with-T, and OLD all-ones / NEW 0), so keep-old, force-0 and force-1 all
     change the word.  This is a UNION across observations, not a property of every decline.
     Named equivalents: bit 4 polarity 0 and bit 4 on accept (every modelled mode and label
     has bit 4 set, so keep-old / force-1 equal the right answer there), and T
     polarity 0 against OLD T = 1 (the guest runs ARM code, so OLD T is always 0; keep-old T is
     caught at polarity 1).  The latch is SET for every decline after a session's first.
  G2 every msr_imm label (FIQ IRQ SVC ABT UND SYS) declines label ^ 0x1f, which differs in all
     five mode bits; the restore labels decline the same value as the SPSR.
  G3 all 25 unmodelled values are declined and all 7 modelled modes accepted on EACH path.
  G4 arm_pop's enclosing cells {S, non-S} x {PC, no PC}, all four, in grid pop.
  The hand-written rows below remain (G5): label x latch x outcome (src), tbit (T with the latch
  clear, by `print cpsr'), encl / encl2 (the DPI enclosing cells), msr wide, msr lbl.  Sessions:
  the probe prints ARMMSRMODE_SESSIONS.

CONTRACT (floor clause (e)) -- the edits this detector is DESIGNED to catch:
  * exit(1) reinstated at Save's default: (dbg save, dbg both, latch rpt) or Load's (dbg both,
    latch rpt);
  * the helper answering 1 always (kill 14/15/1e), wrong on ANY of the 32 mode values on ANY path
    -- narrowed (e.g. `& 0x17' admits 0x18-0x1a), widened by any bit 31:5, or dropped (grid *:
    G3 drives all 25 unmodelled and all 7 modelled values through each path; src rows, tbit,
    msr wide);
  * the guard dropped at msr_imm (kill rows), arm_pop (rst ldm, src pop x FIQ IRQ ABT UND SVC) or
    DPI (rst movs, zero spsr, src dpi x FIQ IRQ ABT UND SVC), or restricted to ONE label, or to
    one unmodelled value such as 0x14 (src rows decline 0x0f), or compiled into the MOV instance
    only (encl2: `subs pc,lr');
  * the msr_imm guard skipped at one current-mode label (msr lbl: a decline from each of FIQ IRQ
    ABT UND SYS; latch rpt: SVC with the latch set);
  * the msr_imm decline clearing fewer than all five mode bits at ANY label (grid msr: G2
    declines label ^ 0x1f at all six labels; kill 03, encl2), or clearing MORE (`& ~0x3f', T lost:
    tbit, grid msr); the restore keep-mode mask letting any SPSR mode bit through (grid dpi/pop:
    G2; src rows);
  * a restore guard made unconditional, or unconditional for one label, or skipped / forced for one
    label x latch-state cell (src rows: all 40 cells; acc movs, acc ldm);
  * a WHOLE-OPERATION decline in msr_imm instead of field-only (the `if=' pin on kill rows; msr
    wide);
  * a decline OR accept taking ANY bit 31:5 from the old CPSR, or forcing it to 0 or to 1,
    with the latch clear or set (grid *: G1 complementary pairs; src R2/R3; tbit; msr wide) --
    this replaces the repair-3 sentence "the declined SPSR has N Z I F SET while the CPSR it
    replaces has them CLEAR" (FALSE for N and Z) and repair 4's "at EVERY decline, every restored
    bit differs" (FALSE for T at src R2/R3 and msr wide: it held only as a UNION);
  * any two of the five latch sites sharing a bit (latch rpt: all five in one process; also
    dbg both, latch ind);
  * a latch that never SUPPRESSES (`|=' -> `&=', or the `|=' line deleted), or a repair applied
    only while its latch is clear (latch rpt: every site visited TWICE; src rows per label);
  * the enclosing predicates widened in ANY of their cells: arm_pop's `if (return_flag)' (grid
    pop: all four {S, non-S} x {PC, no PC}; also encl, encl2), the DPI template's
    `rd == PC' compare (encl: non-S `add r0,pc'; encl2: S `adds r0,pc'), and the restore's
    `#ifdef A__S' (encl: non-S `add pc,r6,#0' -- NOT `mov pc,lr', which the decoder turns into
    `ret' and so never reaches the DPI template).
NOT CLAIMED, disclosed: DPI opcode instances other than MOV and SUB (graded (e): the opcode
flows into nothing the repair reads).  The msr_imm comment's "only the LOCAL mask changes:
ic->arg[] is a cached translation" is NOT pinned, in either form.  Every re-execution in this
probe follows a debugger INTERACTION (to set r1, or to dump), and debugger() clears every dyntrans
translation when it interacts (src/debugger/debugger.c, "Clear all dyntrans translations"; it
returns before that clear only while a `step N' count is still running), so each execution is
re-translated from the instruction word and a write to ic->arg[] never reaches the next one.
MEASURED: M_msr_argmask -- the decline also writes `ic->arg[1] &= ~ARM_FLAG_MODE' -- scores 47/47
(artifacts/armmsrmodekill/killtable5_20261008/kt1b/).  Repair 5's text said the grid's
re-executions of `msr cpsr_fsxc,r1' pinned the register form: FALSE, for that reason (corrected
2026-10-09).  A guest LOOP that re-executes ONE translated MSR inside a single `step N', changing
the register between passes, would pin it; not built.  Per-CPU latch isolation (one CPU only); exception entry as a
Save-backstop route (no row); T-bit / execution-state coherence after a T-set restore beyond the
CPSR word itself; the USR/SYS `LDM {pc}^' and `MSR SPSR' paths that stop the CPU (filed
separately).  The grid, tbit and `print cpsr' readbacks are rung-3 debugger observations.  The
`msr wide' accept 0xffffffd1 sets operand bits 31:6, 4 and 0 only (bits 3:1 are clear: the grid's
accepts and c_17 / c_1b / c_1f carry them) -- repair 4's header said "bits 31:6, 4:0".

NAMED-RED tally: HEAD = 18/47, RED exactly the 29 rows kill 14, kill 15, save 2s, kill 1e,
kill 03, rst movs, rst ldm, zero spsr, dbg save, dbg both, latch ind, latch rpt, the ten src *
rows, msr lbl, tbit, encl2, msr wide, grid msr, grid dpi, grid pop, CONTROL=OK.  `encl' drives no
unmodelled mode and `grid cov' is static, so both are GREEN on HEAD.  `save 2s' on HEAD is the
SAME Load death as `kill 14' (EOF on the first MSR) -- it is not an independent HEAD reproduction
of Save; nor is `dbg both' a HEAD reproduction of Load (HEAD dies in Save first).  47/47 on HEAD
is a broken vehicle, not success.

```

 /*
+ *  arm_mode_is_modelled():
+ *
+ *  #562: 1 if M[4:0] of `mode' is one of the seven modes this model banks
+ *  (10 11 12 13 17 1b 1f), else 0.  Its case list MUST stay equal to the
+ *  case lists of arm_save_register_bank() and arm_load_register_bank()
+ *  below: the three switches together define "modelled", and those two
+ *  default: arms are reached only for a mode this returns 0 for.  Extern,
+ *  not static: the DPI template is compiled as its own object.
+ */
+int arm_mode_is_modelled(uint32_t mode)
+{
+	switch (mode & ARM_FLAG_MODE) {
+	case ARM_MODE_USR32:
+	case ARM_MODE_SYS32:
+	case ARM_MODE_FIQ32:
+	case ARM_MODE_IRQ32:
+	case ARM_MODE_SVC32:
+	case ARM_MODE_ABT32:
+	case ARM_MODE_UND32:
+		return 1;
+	default:return 0;
+	}
+}
+
+

+++ b/src/cpus/cpu_arm_instr.c

 		mask &= 0xff000000;
 	}
 
+	/*
+	 *  #562: a mode-field write naming a mode this model does not bank
+	 *  (reserved, or 26-bit) used to reach arm_load_register_bank()'s
+	 *  exit(1).  The architecture makes the result UNPREDICTABLE (DDI
+	 *  0100I A2.5.7; ARMv7 lets it be UNDEFINED) and forbids halting.
+	 *  Declining the MODE FIELD only, with the rest of the write applied,
+	 *  is this emulator's POLICY, not an architectural requirement.  Only
+	 *  the local mask changes: ic->arg[] is a cached translation.
+	 */
+	if ((mask & ARM_FLAG_MODE) && !arm_mode_is_modelled(ic->arg[0])) {
+		if (!(cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned & ARM_MODEWARN_MSR)) {
+			cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned |= ARM_MODEWARN_MSR;
+			fatal("[ msr_imm: mode 0x%02x is not modelled; mode field"
+			    " not written, other fields are (once) ]\n",
+			    (int)(ic->arg[0] & ARM_FLAG_MODE));
+		}
+		mask &= ~ARM_FLAG_MODE;
+	}
+
 	int switch_register_banks = (mask & ARM_FLAG_MODE) &&
 	    ((cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE) !=
 	    (ic->arg[0] & ARM_FLAG_MODE));
@@ -1799,6 +1818,22 @@
 			return;
 		}
 
+		/*  #562: an SPSR naming an unmodelled mode (an MSR SPSR_c of a
+		    reserved value, or an SPSR never written: zero = mode 0)
+		    keeps the CURRENT mode in M[4:0]; every other bit comes from
+		    the SPSR.  Same POLICY as msr_imm.  No return here: the PC
+		    load below must still run.  */
+		if (!arm_mode_is_modelled(new_cpsr)) {
+			if (!(cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned & ARM_MODEWARN_POP)) {
+				cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned |= ARM_MODEWARN_POP;
+				fatal("[ arm_pop: SPSR mode 0x%02x is not modelled;"
+				    " mode kept (once) ]\n",
+				    (int)(new_cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE));
+			}
+			new_cpsr = (new_cpsr & ~ARM_FLAG_MODE) |
+			    (cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE);
+		}
+
 		switch_register_banks = (cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE) !=
 		    (new_cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE);
 
--- a/src/cpus/cpu_arm_instr_dpi.c
+++ b/src/cpus/cpu_arm_instr_dpi.c

 		}
+		/*  #562: an SPSR naming an unmodelled mode keeps the CURRENT mode
+		    in M[4:0]; the value assigned to CPSR below takes all other
+		    bits from the SPSR (msr_imm's POLICY) -- though T is forced

## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

A semantics change re-binds the floor under a new meaning, and the prior pass-2 cannot have validated that new meaning. **Pass-2 owed.**

The brief: \"a detector row that stores 8 bytes at in-window +0x08 and greps the array name and index\". I'll treat it as: the row performs an 8-byte store at in-window offset 0x08 (delivery path unspecified in the packet) and greps (log output, presumably the debug() line) for the array name and the index. The natural oracle: fix writes halfwords to indices 4..7 at +0x08; buggy code writes 1..4. The anchor index is presumably 4 (first halfword's landing) \u2014 but index 4 is in BOTH traces (overlap!). So:\n\n- If the grep is `re_tex[4]` + value-of-first-halfword: discriminates \u2713 (buggy puts 4th halfword at 4).\n- If the grep is `re_tex[4]` without value: VACUOUS \u2014 passes the full revert `>>1 \u2192 >>3` under BOTH A and B. The overlap at the anchor makes the oracle blind. This is a hole SHARED by A and B, and it's in the ORACLE, not the position. The one-seat-found A-hole (delete alignment conjunct) is additional to this.\n- If the grep anchors index 5/6/7 or the full set {4,5,6,7}: discriminates \u2713 (buggy writes {1,2,3,4}).\n\nThe packet says \"greps the array name and index\" (singular index) \u2014 so plausibly ONE index. If that index is 4, the oracle is vacuous as specified. I should flag: the anchor must be an index OUTSIDE the overlap ({5,6,7}) or include the value. Otherwise even the plain revert passes. This is a defect in the proposed oracle independent of A/B \u2014 worth stating as my Q4 headline, THEN answer B's analogous hole.\n\nUnder B, analogous hole (given a DISCRIMINATING oracle anchored at +0x08 only): the smallest edit that reverts the shift's behavior at all OTHER offsets while passing at +0x08: change the index expression to agree at 0x08 and disagree elsewhere. Smallest token-wise: `>> 1` \u2192 `>> 3) + 3` is not smaller; hmm what about just `>> 3` + something? Let's find the minimal-diff candidates:\n\n(i) `((relative_addr & 0xff) >> 1) & 0xff` \u2192 `((relative_addr & 0xff) >> 3) + 3` \u2014 tokens changed: `1` \u2192 `3) + 3` \u2014 adds tokens. Not minimal.\n\n(ii) Revert to `>> 3` and change the LOOP to compensate at the anchor? The loop is shared.\n\n(iii) `>> 1` \u2192 `>> 3` with `tlbi++` \u2192 `tlbi += 4`? At +0x08: base 1, writes 1, 5, 9, 13 \u2014 the SET is {1,5,9,13} vs fix {4,5,6,7}: index 5 present in both! If oracle greps index 5 alone, this passes! Ha. But if it greps value@5: fix puts 2nd halfword at 5; edit puts 2nd halfword at 5 too (1\u2192first, 5\u2192second, 9\u2192third, 13\u2192fourth)! Wait: edit: tlbi starts 1, writes re_tex[1]=hw0, then tlbi += 4 \u2192 re_tex[5]=hw1, re_tex[9]=hw2, re_tex[13]=hw3. Fix: re_tex[4]=hw0, [5]=hw1, [6]=hw2, [7]=hw3. If oracle greps name+index 5 (+value hw1): BOTH match! If oracle greps index 4+value: edit fails (fix has 4=hw0; edit has no write at 4 \u2014 the ORIGINAL buggy value remains... wait, under the edit, was index 4 written? No. The grep for `re_tex[4]` in the LOG after the store \u2014 the debug prints only what was stored this time? \"a debug() on the next line\" \u2014 inside the loop presumably, printing each stored halfword. So the log shows only stored entries. Edit log: 1,5,9,13. Fix log: 4,5,6,7.) So the discriminating power depends entirely on which index is grepped. The brief says THE index (one). Whatever single index k is grepped, an escaping edit needs to write hw_i at index k for the i the oracle expects. Since the oracle stores 8 bytes = 4 halfwords, and the escape controls both base and stride (via tlbi++ changes) \u2014 the escape space is large. With base b and stride s, writes (b, b+s, b+2s, b+3s). Fix: (4,5,6,7). For any single anchored (index, value) pair (k, hw_i), the escape `>> 3` with stride 4: (1,5,9,13) covers (5, hw1) \u2014 i.e., if the oracle anchors the SECOND halfword's landing (index 5), the `>>3 + tlbi+=4` escape passes. If it anchors the FIRST (4, hw0), that escape fails, but `((x>>3)+3)` = (4,5,6,7) \u2014 identical to fix at the anchor! And that one reverts ALL other offsets (at 0x10: 5..8 vs fix 8..11 \u2014 wait (0x10>>3)+3 = 2+3 = 5, writes 5,6,7,8; fix writes 8,9,10,11 \u2014 overlap at 8! Hmm interesting, partial overlaps everywhere). Whatever \u2014 the point stands: a single-offset, single-index oracle can be satisfied by a family of expressions that revert the shift's semantics elsewhere. The smallest such edit for the likely anchor (4, hw0): `>> 1` \u2192 `>> 3) + 3` OR `>> 3` with loop stride 4 IF anchored at (5,hw1). Both are two-ish-token edits. So YES, B has an analogous hole \u2014 BUT with two mitigating differences: (a) the escaping edits are not the plain revert; a lazy future editor flipping `1`\u2192`3` gets CAUGHT (the plain revert fails the oracle), whereas under A the demonstrated escape IS the lazy edit (delete a conjunct \u2014 a natural \"cleanup\" that a future round might do believing the invariant). The B escapes require deliberately crafting an index expression that agrees at the anchor \u2014 that's adversarial, not accidental. Revert-risk is dominated by accidental/ordinary edits; B's oracle catches the ordinary revert; A's oracle misses the ordinary deletion (conjunct-deletion is exactly the kind of edit \"simplification\" rounds do). (b) Under B, the escaping edits are all still BOUNDS-SAFE at every offset? Check `>>3)+3`: max (0xdf>>3)+3 = 30, writes \u2264 33 \u2713 in bounds. `>>3` stride 4: 1,5,9,13... base max 27 \u2192 27,31,35,39 \u2713. So B's holes cannot reintroduce OOB; A's hole (deleting the conjunct, keeping >>1) reintroduces the OOB exactly. Actually hmm, wait: under A, the escape deletes the alignment conjunct \u2014 with >>1 still in place, unaligned put d \u2192 OOB. Yes. So: A's hole reopens the safety defect; B's hole only mis-indexes. In a hardening fork, the safety-relevant hole is worse. So B's oracle-hole is strictly less dangerous.\n\nBUT the shared vacuity risk (overlap at index 4) applies to both: if the oracle greps name+index where index=4 sans value, the PLAIN revert passes under both A and B. Must fix the oracle: anchor on value, or on an index in {5,6,7} \u2014 actually anchor on ALL FOUR (index,value) pairs, and ideally add a SECOND offset (e.g., +0x10) to kill the affine-escape family (two anchors pin base+stride... well, pin the map at two points; a crafted expression can still interpolate, e.g., piecewise, but each added anchor raises the cost). Honestly, no finite behavioral anchor set at aligned offsets can distinguish `>>1` from `(x>>3)<<2` since they AGREE on all aligned offsets \u2014 but that's fine, because (x>>3)<<2 is not a revert on the reachable set (identical behavior on all aligned stores; it differs only on the unreachable/put d set, where it's SAFER: max 108+3 = 111 in bounds \u2014 wait let me recompute: (0xdf>>3)<<2 = 27<<2 = 108, writes 108..111 \u2014 in bounds \u2713, and at aligned offsets identical to fix). So the affine escape that survives ALL aligned anchors is behaviorally IDENTICAL to the fix on every guest-reachable store AND bounds-safe on put d \u2014 i.e., it's not a revert at all; the oracle correctly doesn't care. Lovely \u2014 that closes the loop: under B with aligned-only anchors, any edit passing all anchors is either the fix's behavior on the reachable set or safer. The residual unanchored surface is exactly the put d/unaligned fringe, which B has decided (via the LINEAR disposition) to file rather than guard. Consistent.\n\nUnder A, by contrast, the un-anchored surface includes the guard itself, and its deletion is both natural and undetectable. So on Q4: B's hole exists in principle (single-anchor affine escapes) but every escape is either caught (plain revert) or non-behavioral on the reachable set; A's hole is a natural deletion that reopens the OOB. B is strictly better on oracle-durability. Plus the shared vacuity-at-index-4 defect must be fixed in the oracle itself regardless.\n\nHmm, wait \u2014 one more consideration for Q4 under B: the comment. #!/usr/bin/env python3
"""armmsrmodekill -- RUNG-3 WITNESS.  One privileged MSR ends the PROCESS on `-E testarm'.

BUILT FROM regress/artifacts/armmsrmodekill/WITNESSSPEC_REWRITE_grok_20260917.txt, which is a
REWRITE and not the original 16-row spec plus eight sticky notes.  That spec's amendments file
carries a SUPERSEDED banner; do not build from it.

REPAIRED after regress/artifacts/armmsrmodekill/PROBEATTACK_grok_20260917.txt (the attack on the
never-run first draft) -- see the `REPAIR' notes inline.  Summary of the repairs:
  R1  dump parsing: aligned base + little-endian assembly (read_mem32() in arm_bdt_probe.py,
      sw() in arm_flags_probe.py).  The first draft already carried both; kept.
  R2  EARLY/LATE SPLIT.  Every MSR session dumps the 0x9000 line TWICE (the arming dump of DEST
      before the stimulus, the result dump after it).  A first-match parse returns the EARLY
      line, where every post-MSR slot is still POISON -- so a correct fix would have read
      `nofire' on every kill row.  An `@MARK' pseudo-command becomes `print 0x5eed5eed' and
      the transcript is split at that token; arming is parsed only from before it, results
      only from after it.
  R3  `save 2s' plant and classifier agree, on the spec's own slots: return CPSR at DEST+8,
      SP at DEST+12, both inside the one dumped line.  The first draft planted SP at DEST+16
      and never dumped 0x9010, so ` sp' could never appear -- the row was ALWAYS red.  `rt'
      requires mode == 0x13 at the return slot, not mere existence.
  R4  the plant-dump of the MSR slot is SCORED: every MSR session dumps its whole planted
      stream before the stimulus and checks each word against WORDS, plus DEST == 0xd3 (the
      spec's "every later session repeats DEST==0xd3 + plant-dump").  A failure renders the
      row `unarmed', never `dead'.  This is what separates "died at the MSR" from "the MSR was
      never the instruction that ran".
  R5  CONTROL=DEAD stops the probe: `enc ok' red stops before any guest session; `live' or
      `pc adv' red stops before `armed'; `armed' red stops before any kill row.
  R6  `enc ok' note quoted the removed (w & 0x0FB0F000) mask; it now states the field-nibble
      test the code performs.  f_14 additionally checks rot == 0.
  R7  liveness: EOF on the pty is recorded; on EOF the child is reaped with a long deadline,
      without EOF with a short one (a live emulator never exits, and every live session used to
      burn the full 12 s).  The SIGKILL tail now READS THE STATUS: if waitpid shows the child
      exited on its own (not by our SIGKILL), alive is False.
  R8  canary accept rows also check arrival SP == 0 (spec: "arrive m=XX SP=0"), and `acc usr'
      now passes sp_at so a Load-skip (SP still BOOT_SP in USR32) reds it.

THE DEFECT, verified by reading cpu_arm.c at HEAD ef577140 -- PRE-#562; #562 replaced both exits
with latched backstops, so these lines describe the unfixed tree only:

    cpu_arm.c:606-608   arm_load_register_bank: default: fatal("...unimplemented mode %i"); exit(1);
    cpu_arm.c:559-561   arm_save_register_bank: default: same

`0xE321F014' is `MSR CPSR_c, #0x14' -- mode 0x14 is a RESERVED ARM mode, so the Load arm's
`default:' is reached and the process dies.  `%i' prints DECIMAL: `unimplemented mode 20'.

*** POLARITY: WANT IS POST-FIX CORRECT.  A PRE FAIL ON A DEFECT ROW *IS* THE REPRODUCTION. ***
*** ORACLE: EOF ALONE => DEAD.  THE TEXT IS DIAGNOSIS, NEVER HALF THE TRIGGER. ***

#562 THE FIX AND WHAT THIS PROBE NOW DETECTS.  Three guest writers can put an unmodelled mode
(anything but 10 11 12 13 17 1b 1f) into CPSR, and each now DECLINES THE MODE FIELD ONLY, with a
`(once)' announce naming its site: msr_imm (`MSR CPSR_c'), arm_pop's `LDM {..pc}^' SPSR restore,
and the DPI S-to-PC SPSR restore (`MOVS pc,..', `SUBS pc,lr,..').  The restores keep the CURRENT
mode in M[4:0] and assign every other bit from the SPSR (DPI later forces T for an odd destination
PC; not driven here).  The Save/Load `default:' arms are BACKSTOPS (latched announce, return, no
exit(1)), reached only once an unmodelled mode is ALREADY in CPSR, which no guest instruction can
now install -- only a debugger `cpsr=' assignment.  After such a plant Save's default: is reached
by a guest `MSR CPSR_c' to a modelled mode (dbg save), a DPI S-to-PC (dbg both, latch rpt) or
exception entry; Load's default: ONLY by a DPI S-to-PC executed in the planted mode (dbg both,
latch rpt).  An `LDM {..pc}^' from a planted mode stops the CPU at arm_pop's selector default:
first (pre-existing; not this fix).  POST-FIX the guest is in SVC32 with the I/F bits the write
set -- `#0x14' clears I and F -- NOT in a reserved mode.  Rows added by #562: kill 1e, kill 03,
rst movs, rst ldm, zero spsr, acc movs, acc ldm, dbg save, dbg both, latch ind, latch rpt, src
dpi/src pop x {fiq irq abt und svc}, msr lbl, encl, tbit, encl2, msr wide, grid msr, grid dpi,
grid pop, grid cov, the `if=' (I/F) pin on
kill 14 / kill 15, and ` sp0' (an actual zero SP readback) on the rows whose SP is a never-loaded
bank.

MODES 0x00-0x0F ARE NOW CLAIMED.  This header used to BAN `MSR CPSR_c, #0' as "the 26-bit hole,
a different defect".  The helper declines 0x00-0x0F like any other unmodelled value, and
`zero spsr' drives exactly mode 0x00 (an SPSR never written is zero: cpu_new zero-fills).

THE GENERATED GRID (repair 5; owner ruling 2026-10-08).  The restore/MSR coverage is GENERATED
from tables (grid_obs(): labels, MODELLED, UNMODELLED, complementary value pairs), not written row
by row: `grid msr', `grid dpi', `grid pop' run every generated observation and compare the WHOLE
CPSR word; `grid cov' re-derives the coverage table from the same generator and reds on any gap.
  G1 every (path, outcome, bit 0..31, polarity) cell has an observation whose result bit must
     equal the SPSR / operand value while the OLD CPSR's bit differs (complementary pairs:
     OLD 0 / NEW all-ones-with-T, and OLD all-ones / NEW 0), so keep-old, force-0 and force-1 all
     change the word.  This is a UNION across observations, not a property of every decline.
     Named equivalents: bit 4 polarity 0 and bit 4 on accept (every modelled mode and label
     has bit 4 set, so keep-old / force-1 equal the right answer there), and T
     polarity 0 against OLD T = 1 (the guest runs ARM code, so OLD T is always 0; keep-old T is
     caught at polarity 1).  The latch is SET for every decline after a session's first.
  G2 every msr_imm label (FIQ IRQ SVC ABT UND SYS) declines label ^ 0x1f, which differs in all
     five mode bits; the restore labels decline the same value as the SPSR.
  G3 all 25 unmodelled values are declined and all 7 modelled modes accepted on EACH path.
  G4 arm_pop's enclosing cells {S, non-S} x {PC, no PC}, all four, in grid pop.
  The hand-written rows below remain (G5): label x latch x outcome (src), tbit (T with the latch
  clear, by `print cpsr'), encl / encl2 (the DPI enclosing cells), msr wide, msr lbl.  Sessions:
  the probe prints ARMMSRMODE_SESSIONS.

CONTRACT (floor clause (e)) -- the edits this detector is DESIGNED to catch:
  * exit(1) reinstated at Save's default: (dbg save, dbg both, latch rpt) or Load's (dbg both,
    latch rpt);
  * the helper answering 1 always (kill 14/15/1e), wrong on ANY of the 32 mode values on ANY path
    -- narrowed (e.g. `& 0x17' admits 0x18-0x1a), widened by any bit 31:5, or dropped (grid *:
    G3 drives all 25 unmodelled and all 7 modelled values through each path; src rows, tbit,
    msr wide);
  * the guard dropped at msr_imm (kill rows), arm_pop (rst ldm, src pop x FIQ IRQ ABT UND SVC) or
    DPI (rst movs, zero spsr, src dpi x FIQ IRQ ABT UND SVC), or restricted to ONE label, or to
    one unmodelled value such as 0x14 (src rows decline 0x0f), or compiled into the MOV instance
    only (encl2: `subs pc,lr');
  * the msr_imm guard skipped at one current-mode label (msr lbl: a decline from each of FIQ IRQ
    ABT UND SYS; latch rpt: SVC with the latch set);
  * the msr_imm decline clearing fewer than all five mode bits at ANY label (grid msr: G2
    declines label ^ 0x1f at all six labels; kill 03, encl2), or clearing MORE (`& ~0x3f', T lost:
    tbit, grid msr); the restore keep-mode mask letting any SPSR mode bit through (grid dpi/pop:
    G2; src rows);
  * a restore guard made unconditional, or unconditional for one label, or skipped / forced for one
    label x latch-state cell (src rows: all 40 cells; acc movs, acc ldm);
  * a WHOLE-OPERATION decline in msr_imm instead of field-only (the `if=' pin on kill rows; msr
    wide);
  * a decline OR accept taking ANY bit 31:5 from the old CPSR, or forcing it to 0 or to 1,
    with the latch clear or set (grid *: G1 complementary pairs; src R2/R3; tbit; msr wide) --
    this replaces the repair-3 sentence "the declined SPSR has N Z I F SET while the CPSR it
    replaces has them CLEAR" (FALSE for N and Z) and repair 4's "at EVERY decline, every restored
    bit differs" (FALSE for T at src R2/R3 and msr wide: it held only as a UNION);
  * any two of the five latch sites sharing a bit (latch rpt: all five in one process; also
    dbg both, latch ind);
  * a latch that never SUPPRESSES (`|=' -> `&=', or the `|=' line deleted), or a repair applied
    only while its latch is clear (latch rpt: every site visited TWICE; src rows per label);
  * the enclosing predicates widened in ANY of their cells: arm_pop's `if (return_flag)' (grid
    pop: all four {S, non-S} x {PC, no PC}; also encl, encl2), the DPI template's
    `rd == PC' compare (encl: non-S `add r0,pc'; encl2: S `adds r0,pc'), and the restore's
    `#ifdef A__S' (encl: non-S `add pc,r6,#0' -- NOT `mov pc,lr', which the decoder turns into
    `ret' and so never reaches the DPI template).
NOT CLAIMED, disclosed: DPI opcode instances other than MOV and SUB (graded (e): the opcode
flows into nothing the repair reads).  The msr_imm comment's "only the LOCAL mask changes:
ic->arg[] is a cached translation" is NOT pinned, in either form.  Every re-execution in this
probe follows a debugger INTERACTION (to set r1, or to dump), and debugger() clears every dyntrans
translation when it interacts (src/debugger/debugger.c, "Clear all dyntrans translations"; it
returns before that clear only while a `step N' count is still running), so each execution is
re-translated from the instruction word and a write to ic->arg[] never reaches the next one.
MEASURED: M_msr_argmask -- the decline also writes `ic->arg[1] &= ~ARM_FLAG_MODE' -- scores 47/47
(artifacts/armmsrmodekill/killtable5_20261008/kt1b/).  Repair 5's text said the grid's
re-executions of `msr cpsr_fsxc,r1' pinned the register form: FALSE, for that reason (corrected
2026-10-09).  A guest LOOP that re-executes ONE translated MSR inside a single `step N', changing
the register between passes, would pin it; not built.  Per-CPU latch isolation (one CPU only); exception entry as a
Save-backstop route (no row); T-bit / execution-state coherence after a T-set restore beyond the
CPSR word itself; the USR/SYS `LDM {pc}^' and `MSR SPSR' paths that stop the CPU (filed
separately).  The grid, tbit and `print cpsr' readbacks are rung-3 debugger observations.  The
`msr wide' accept 0xffffffd1 sets operand bits 31:6, 4 and 0 only (bits 3:1 are clear: the grid's
accepts and c_17 / c_1b / c_1f carry them) -- repair 4's header said "bits 31:6, 4:0".

NAMED-RED tally: HEAD = 18/47, RED exactly the 29 rows kill 14, kill 15, save 2s, kill 1e,
kill 03, rst movs, rst ldm, zero spsr, dbg save, dbg both, latch ind, latch rpt, the ten src *
rows, msr lbl, tbit, encl2, msr wide, grid msr, grid dpi, grid pop, CONTROL=OK.  `encl' drives no
unmodelled mode and `grid cov' is static, so both are GREEN on HEAD.  `save 2s' on HEAD is the
SAME Load death as `kill 14' (EOF on the first MSR) -- it is not an independent HEAD reproduction
of Save; nor is `dbg both' a HEAD reproduction of Load (HEAD dies in Save first).  47/47 on HEAD
is a broken vehicle, not success.

Stay on `step', never `continue' (IRQ dispatch is inside `if (!single_step)').
"""
