def classify(early, late, alive, marked, full, cpsr_at=DEST + 4, sp_at=None, rt_at=None,
             once_key=None, pin_if=False, backstops=False, declines=False):
    """The got string.  EOF ALONE => dead.  Arming is checked first, from the EARLY half.
    #562: once_key names the announcing site; pin_if adds the CPSR I/F pair (field-only vs
    whole-operation decline); backstops adds ` save' / ` load' per Save/Load announce."""
    if not marked or not arming(early, full):
        return "unarmed"
    if not alive:
        return "dead"
    cpsr = dumped(late, cpsr_at)
    if cpsr is None or cpsr == POISON:
        return "nofire"
    got = "m=%02x" % (cpsr & 0x1F)
    if pin_if:
        got += " if=%d" % ((cpsr >> 6) & 3)
    if sp_at is not None:
        v = dumped(late, sp_at)
        #  #562 pass 2: ` sp0' is an ACTUAL zero readback (a never-loaded bank), not merely
        #  "not BOOT_SP" -- a missing or POISON slot now renders neither token.
        if v == BOOT_SP:
            got += " sp"
        elif v == 0:
            got += " sp0"
    t = early + late
    if (announced(t, once_key) if once_key else "(once)" in t):
        got += " once"
    if declines:
        for key, tag in (("msr_imm", "msr"), ("arm_pop", "pop"), ("dpi S-to-pc", "dpi")):
            if announced(t, key):
                got += " " + tag
    if backstops:
        if announced(t, "arm_save_register_bank"):
            got += " save"
        if announced(t, "arm_load_register_bank"):
            got += " load"
    if rt_at is not None:
        v = dumped(late, rt_at)
        if v not in (None, POISON) and (v & 0x1F) == 0x13:
            got += " rt"
    return got


def report_dead(why):
    print("arm_msrmode_probe -- binary=%s label=%s machine=testarm" % (BIN, LABEL))
    print("ARMMSRMODE_CONTROL=DEAD")
    print("ARMMSRMODE_RESULT=0/0")
    print("  *** SETUP FAILED (%s) -- the vehicle is not the vehicle.  NOT 'no defect'. ***" % why)
    for nm, got, want, note in rows:
        print("  %-11s got=%-16s want=%-16s %s" % (nm, got, want, note[:56]))
    print("ARMMSRMODE_DONE")
    raise SystemExit(0)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- enc ok  (no guest)
#  MSR immediate:  cond 00110 R 10 field(19:16) 1111 rot(11:8) imm8.  The field nibble is
#  bits 19:16 and a mask cannot substitute for it (0x0FB0F000 kills bit 0x08 and cannot tell
#  the c-form from the f-form).
bad = []
for k, w in WORDS.items():
    if k.startswith("c_"):
        if ((w >> 16) & 0xF) != 0b0001 or (w & 0xFF) != int(k[2:], 16) \
                or ((w >> 8) & 0xF) != 0 or (w & 0x00400000) or (w & 0x0FB0F000) != 0x0320F000:
            bad.append(k)
    if k == "f_14":
        if ((w >> 16) & 0xF) != 0b1000 or (w & 0xFF) != 0x14 or ((w >> 8) & 0xF) != 0 \
                or (w & 0x00400000) or (w & 0x0FB0F000) != 0x0320F000:
            bad.append(k)
    if k.startswith("s_"):
        #  #562: the SPSR form -- same frame, field nibble 0001, bit 22 (R) SET.
        if ((w >> 16) & 0xF) != 0b0001 or (w & 0xFF) != int(k[2:], 16) \
                or ((w >> 8) & 0xF) != 0 or not (w & 0x00400000) \
                or (w & 0x0FB0F000) != 0x0320F000:
            bad.append(k)
    if k == "movs_pc_lr":
        #  data-processing MOV (opcode 1101), S set, Rd = 15, Rm = 14, no shift
        if (w >> 21) & 0xF != 0xD or not (w >> 20) & 1 or (w >> 12) & 0xF != 15 \
                or w & 0xFFF != 14 or (w >> 25) & 7 != 0:
            bad.append(k)
    if k in ("ldm_r6_pc_s", "mov_lr_8100", "add_lr_40", "sf_c0"):
        #  #562 pass 2: whole-word field decode against the intended operation.
        ok = {"ldm_r6_pc_s": (w >> 25) & 7 == 0b100 and (w >> 20) & 1 and (w >> 22) & 1
              and not (w >> 21) & 1 and (w >> 16) & 0xF == 6 and w & 0xFFFF == 0x8000,
              "mov_lr_8100": (w >> 25) & 7 == 1 and (w >> 21) & 0xF == 0xD
              and not (w >> 20) & 1 and (w >> 12) & 0xF == 14
              and (((w & 0xFF) >> (2 * ((w >> 8) & 0xF))) | ((w & 0xFF)
                   << (32 - 2 * ((w >> 8) & 0xF)))) & 0xFFFFFFFF == TGT,
              "add_lr_40": (w >> 25) & 7 == 1 and (w >> 21) & 0xF == 0x4
              and not (w >> 20) & 1 and (w >> 16) & 0xF == 14 and (w >> 12) & 0xF == 14
              and w & 0xFFF == 0x40,
              "sf_c0": (w & 0x0FB0F000) == 0x0320F000 and (w & 0x00400000)
              and ((w >> 16) & 0xF) == 0b1000 and ((w >> 8) & 0xF) == 1 and (w & 0xFF) == 3,
              }[k]
        if not ok:
            bad.append(k)
    if k in ("sf_ff", "ss_ff", "sx_ff", "cf_0", "msr_fsxc_r0", "msr_fsxc_r1", "adds_r0_pc_0",
             "ldm_r5_r0_s", "subs_pc_lr_0"):
        #  #562 repair 4: decode the closure words field by field (rotated imm value, R, field mask,
        #  opcode/S/Rn/Rd, LDM bits) against the operation each key names.
        rot = (w >> 8) & 0xF
        val = ((w & 0xFF) >> (2 * rot) | (w & 0xFF) << (32 - 2 * rot)) & 0xFFFFFFFF
        msri = (w & 0x0FB0F000) == 0x0320F000
        ok = {"sf_ff": msri and (w >> 22) & 1 and (w >> 16) & 0xF == 8 and val == 0xFF000000,
              "ss_ff": msri and (w >> 22) & 1 and (w >> 16) & 0xF == 4 and val == 0x00FF0000,
              "sx_ff": msri and (w >> 22) & 1 and (w >> 16) & 0xF == 2 and val == 0x0000FF00,
              "cf_0": msri and not (w >> 22) & 1 and (w >> 16) & 0xF == 0xE and val == 0,
              "msr_fsxc_r0": w & 0x0FFFFFF0 == 0x012FF000 and w & 0xF == 0,
              "msr_fsxc_r1": w & 0x0FFFFFF0 == 0x012FF000 and w & 0xF == 1,
              "adds_r0_pc_0": (w >> 25) & 7 == 1 and (w >> 21) & 0xF == 4 and (w >> 20) & 1
              and (w >> 16) & 0xF == 15 and (w >> 12) & 0xF == 0 and val == 0,
              "subs_pc_lr_0": (w >> 25) & 7 == 1 and (w >> 21) & 0xF == 2 and (w >> 20) & 1
              and (w >> 16) & 0xF == 14 and (w >> 12) & 0xF == 15 and val == 0,
              "ldm_r5_r0_s": (w >> 25) & 7 == 4 and (w >> 20) & 1 and (w >> 22) & 1
              and not (w >> 21) & 1 and (w >> 23) & 1 and not (w >> 24) & 1
              and (w >> 16) & 0xF == 5 and w & 0xFFFF == 0x0001}[k]
        if not ok or w >> 28 != 0xE:
            bad.append(k)
    if k in ("movs_pc_r5", "add_r5_40", "ldm_r6w_pc_s", "ldm_r5_pc", "add_pc_r6_0", "add_r0_pc_0",
             "str_r0_c", "str_r3_10", "str_r3_14"):
        #  #562 repair 3: cond, class, opcode, S/W/L, Rn, Rd and operand, field by field.
        f = ((w >> 28), (w >> 25) & 7, (w >> 21) & 0xF, (w >> 20) & 1, (w >> 16) & 0xF,
             (w >> 12) & 0xF, w & 0xFFF)
        want = {"movs_pc_r5": (0xE, 0, 0xD, 1, 0, 15, 5),
                "add_r5_40": (0xE, 1, 0x4, 0, 5, 5, 0x40),
                "add_pc_r6_0": (0xE, 1, 0x4, 0, 6, 15, 0),
                "add_r0_pc_0": (0xE, 1, 0x4, 0, 15, 0, 0),
                "str_r0_c": (0xE, 2, 0xC, 0, 4, 0, 0xC),
                "str_r3_10": (0xE, 2, 0xC, 0, 4, 3, 0x10),
                "str_r3_14": (0xE, 2, 0xC, 0, 4, 3, 0x14)}.get(k)
        if k == "ldm_r6w_pc_s":
            ok = (w >> 25) & 7 == 4 and (w >> 20) & 1 and (w >> 22) & 1 and (w >> 21) & 1 \
                and (w >> 23) & 1 and not (w >> 24) & 1 and (w >> 16) & 0xF == 6 \
                and w & 0xFFFF == 0x8000
        elif k == "ldm_r5_pc":
            ok = (w >> 25) & 7 == 4 and (w >> 20) & 1 and not (w >> 22) & 1 \
                and not (w >> 21) & 1 and (w >> 23) & 1 and not (w >> 24) & 1 \
                and (w >> 16) & 0xF == 5 and w & 0xFFFF == 0x8000
        else:
            ok = f == want
        if not ok:
            bad.append(k)
    if k == "ldm_r5_pc_s":
        #  LDM: bits 27:25 = 100, L set, S (bit 22) set, W clear, Rn = 5, list = {pc} only
        if (w >> 25) & 7 != 0b100 or not (w >> 20) & 1 or not (w >> 22) & 1 \
                or (w >> 21) & 1 or (w >> 16) & 0xF != 5 or w & 0xFFFF != 0x8000:
            bad.append(k)
row("enc ok", "clean" if not bad else ",".join(bad), "clean",
    "CONTROL=DEAD. Decoder match on the PLANTED literals: MSR-imm frame (w & 0x0FB0F000) == "
    "0x0320F000, field nibble (w>>16)&0xF = 0001 c-form / 1000 f-form, rot 0, bit 22 clear, imm8 "
    "== the key's mode.")
if bad:
    report_dead("enc ok")

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- S1: live / pc adv
early, late, alive, marked = _session_split(
    prologue() + plant(["mov_r0_2a", "str_r0"])
    + ["pc=0x%x" % CODE, "step 2", dumpcmd(DEST), "print pc"])

    "CONTROL=DEAD. Plant, step and dump executed at all.")

if rows[-2][1] != rows[-2][2] or rows[-1][1] != rows[-1][2]:
    report_dead("live/pc adv")

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- S1b: armed

+/*
+ *  arm_mode_is_modelled():
+ *
+ *  #562: 1 if M[4:0] of `mode' is one of the seven modes this model banks
+ *  (10 11 12 13 17 1b 1f), else 0.  Its case list MUST stay equal to the
+ *  case lists of arm_save_register_bank() and arm_load_register_bank()
+ *  below: the three switches together define "modelled", and those two
+ *  default: arms are reached only for a mode this returns 0 for.  Extern,
+ *  not static: the DPI template is compiled as its own object.
+ */
+int arm_mode_is_modelled(uint32_t mode)
+{
+	switch (mode & ARM_FLAG_MODE) {
+	case ARM_MODE_USR32:
+	case ARM_MODE_SYS32:
+	case ARM_MODE_FIQ32:
+	case ARM_MODE_IRQ32:
+	case ARM_MODE_SVC32:
+	case ARM_MODE_ABT32:
+	case ARM_MODE_UND32:
+		return 1;
+	default:return 0;
+	}
+}
+

  *  arm_save_register_bank():
  */

 		break;
-	default:fatal("arm_save_register_bank: unimplemented mode %i\n",
-		    cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE);
-		exit(1);
+	default:/*  #562: reached only once an unmodelled mode is ALREADY in
+		    CPSR, which only a debugger `cpsr=' assignment can now put
+		    there (msr_imm and both SPSR restores decline it).  After
+		    such a plant, Save runs on it from a guest MSR to a modelled
+		    mode, a DPI S-to-PC return, or exception/IRQ entry; an
+		    LDM {..pc}^ return does not get here -- arm_pop's own
+		    selector default: stops the CPU first (pre-existing).
+		    Backstop: announce once per CPU and return -- no exit(1),
+		    no rollback; r8-r14 are not saved.  */
+		if (!(cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned & ARM_MODEWARN_SAVE)) {
+			cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned |= ARM_MODEWARN_SAVE;
+			fatal("arm_save_register_bank: unimplemented mode %i;"
+			    " registers not saved (once)\n",
+			    cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE);
+		}
+		return;
 	}

+	 *  #562: a mode-field write naming a mode this model does not bank
+	 *  (reserved, or 26-bit) used to reach arm_load_register_bank()'s
+	 *  exit(1).  The architecture makes the result UNPREDICTABLE (DDI

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- #562 repair 3: src dpi / src pop
#  CLASS CLOSURE of label x latch x outcome x masked bits (see the header).  Per label and path,
#  ONE process, four chained restores, each read back:
#    enter the label with I|F set; SPSR := N|Z|0xdf; restore     -> ACCEPT, latch clear  (R1)
#    re-enter it with I|F CLEAR;  SPSR_c := 0xcf;   restore     -> DECLINE, latch clear (R2)
#                                                    restore     -> DECLINE, latch SET   (R3)
#                                 SPSR_c := 0xdf;   restore     -> ACCEPT, latch set    (R4)
#  DPI chains through `movs pc,r5' (lr is banked; r5 is not) with r5 += 0x40 per block; POP chains
#  through `ldmia r6!,{pc}^' over a table of block addresses.  Want R1 = R4 = ffffffdf and R2 = R3
#  = ffffffc0|label (mode kept, every other bit from the SPSR 0xffffffcf; `msr cpsr_fsx,#0' and the
#  label re-entry before each decline leave the old CPSR 0x0000000m), and EXACTLY ONE announce.
BLK = [TGT + 0x40 * i for i in range(4)]     # 0x8100 0x8140 0x8180 0x81c0
for lab, mode in (("fiq", 0x11), ("irq", 0x12), ("abt", 0x17), ("und", 0x1B), ("svc", 0x13)):
    ck = "c_%02x" % (0xC0 | mode)            # first entry: I|F SET (helper operand bits 7:6)
    cc = "c_%02x" % mode                     # re-entry: I|F CLEAR, so the declines' I/F (set in
                                             # the SPSR) can only have come from the SPSR
    for path in ("dpi", "pop"):
        if path == "dpi":

                [ck, "sf_ff", "ss_ff", "sx_ff", "s_df", "movs_pc_r5"],

                                  "movs_pc_r5"]),

                        (BLK[2], ["mrs_r3", "str_r3_c", "add_r5_40", "s_df", "movs_pc_r5"]),
                        (BLK[3], ["mrs_r3", "str_r3_10"])],
                regs=["r5=0x%x" % BLK[0]],
                segments=(((), 26),), late_end=DEST + 0x14)
            key = "dpi S-to-pc"
        else:
            early, late, alive, marked, full = prog_session(
                [ck, "sf_ff", "ss_ff", "sx_ff", "s_df", "ldm_r6w_pc_s"],
                blocks=[(BLK[0], ["mrs_r3", "str_r3_4", "cf_0", cc, "s_cf", "ldm_r6w_pc_s"]),
                        (BLK[1], ["mrs_r3", "str_r3_8", "cf_0", cc, "ldm_r6w_pc_s"]),

        row("src %s %s" % (path, lab), got, "%08x %08x %08x %08x n=1" % (acc, dec, dec, acc),
            "%s from %s: accept, decline, decline (latch set), accept; one announce."

        lab = [l for l in labels if l != m][j % (len(labels) - 1)]
        add("acc", lab, m, 1 + (j % 2))
        j += 1
    if path == "pop":                            # G4 the three non-restoring enclosing cells
        for kind in ("ldm_pc", "ldm_r0_s", "ldm_r0"):
            for pol in (1, 2):
                add(kind, 0x13 if pol == 1 else 0x12, 0x1F, pol)
    return obs


def grid_cov(path):
    """Uncovered (outcome, bit, polarity) cells of path's observations, outside the named
    equivalents.  Covered: some observation whose WANT bit == pol while the alternative differs --
    OLD's bit for every non-mode bit and for accept mode bits, NEW's (the SPSR / operand's) bit
    for decline mode bits.  Named equivalents: bit 4 polarity 0 (no modelled mode or label has it
    clear), bit 4 on ACCEPT at either polarity (OLD's bit 4 is always 1, as is the accepted
    mode's, so keep-old and force-1 equal the right answer), and bit 5 polarity 0 vs OLD (OLD T
    is always 0: the guest runs ARM code)."""
    miss = []
    obs = grid_obs(path)
    for outc in ("acc", "dec"):
        for bit in range(32):
            for pol in (0, 1):
                if (bit == 4 and pol == 0) or (bit == 5 and pol == 0) or                         (bit == 4 and outc == "acc"):
                    continue
                ok = False
                for kind, lab, old, new, want in obs:
                    if kind != outc or ((want >> bit) & 1) != pol:
                        continue
                    alt = new if (outc == "dec" and bit < 5) else old
                    if ((alt >> bit) & 1) != pol:
                        ok = True
                        break
                if not ok:
                    miss.append("%s/b%d/%d" % (outc, bit, pol))
    return miss


GRID_SITE = {"msr": "msr_imm", "dpi": "dpi S-to-pc", "pop": "arm_pop"}
GB0, GB1, GB2, GB3, GTGT = 0x8200, 0x8240, 0x8280, 0x82C0, 0x8300

    segs = [((), 0)]

        bad = ["%d:%08x!=%08x" % (k, v, o[4]) for k, (v, o) in enumerate(zip(vals, obs))
               if v != o[4]]
        t = early + late
        nbank = announces(t, "register_bank")
        got = "%d/%d n=%d" % (len(vals) - len(bad), len(obs), announces(t, GRID_SITE[path]))
        if len(vals) != len(obs):
            got += " read=%d" % len(vals)

        if bad:
            got += " " + " ".join(bad[:2])
            diagnosis.append("grid %s mismatches: %s" % (path, " ".join(bad[:12])))
    row("grid %s" % path, got, "%d/%d n=1" % (len(obs), len(obs)),
        "GENERATED: %d observations (G1-G4) on the %s path; whole-CPSR readback each." %
        (len(obs), path))

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- #562 repair 5: the GENERATED GRID
#  Owner ruling 2026-10-08: the restore/MSR detector rows are GENERATED from the full space, not
#  written by hand.  Three sessions (one per path: msr_imm, DPI S-to-PC, arm_pop), each a list of
#  OBSERVATIONS produced by grid_obs() below.  One observation:
#      debugger:  cpsr=0xd3 (re-arm; a previous result may have T set), pc=<block>, r0=OLD, r1=NEW
#      guest:     msr cpsr_fsxc,r0           -- OLD CPSR, a modelled label, set by a real MSR
#                                               (bank switch and `flags' both correct)
#                 msr spsr_fsxc,r1           -- (restore paths only) SPSR := NEW
#                 the instruction under test -- msr cpsr_fsxc,r1 / movs pc,r2 / ldmia r6,{pc}^
#                                               (pop also: ldmia r6,{pc}, ldmia r6,{r0}^, ldmia r6,{r0})
#      debugger:  print cpsr                 -- the WHOLE result word, compared exactly with WANT
#  WANT is computed: accept -> NEW; decline -> (NEW & ~0x1f) | label; a non-restoring pop form
#  -> OLD.  The first decline in a session runs with the site's latch CLEAR, every later one with
#  it SET; the site must announce exactly once and no Save/Load backstop text may appear.
#
#  G1 per path x outcome x bit 0..31 x polarity: observations come in COMPLEMENTARY PAIRS --
#     p1: OLD = label (bits 31:5 all 0)          NEW = 0xffffffe0 | mode (bits 31:5 all 1, T SET)
#     p2: OLD = 0xffffffc0 | label (31:6 all 1)  NEW = mode (bits 31:5 all 0)
#     so for every non-mode bit both result polarities are observed with the OLD value different
#     (keep-old, force-0 and force-1 all change the word).  T cannot be 1 in OLD (the guest runs
#     ARM code), so T polarity 0 is observed only with OLD T = 0 -- keep-old T is caught at p1.
#  G2 MSR label x mode bit: each label also declines label ^ 0x1f, which differs in EVERY mode bit
#     (and is always unmodelled, since bit 4 is cleared); on the restore paths the same value is
#     the declined SPSR, so a keep-mode mask that lets any SPSR mode bit through changes the word.
#  G3 helper boundary: ALL 25 unmodelled values (0x00-0x0f, 0x14-0x16, 0x18-0x1a, 0x1c-0x1e) are
#     declined on EVERY path, labels cycling; ALL 7 modelled modes are accepted on every path.
#     A narrowed projection (e.g. `& 0x17' admits 0x18-0x1a) or widened one changes some word.
#  G4 pop enclosing cells: {S, non-S} x {PC, no PC} all four, both polarities.
#  The `grid cov' row re-derives the bit x polarity table from the generated observations and
#  reds if any cell outside the named equivalents is uncovered.
MODELLED = (0x10, 0x11, 0x12, 0x13, 0x17, 0x1B, 0x1F)
UNMODELLED = tuple(m for m in range(32) if m not in MODELLED)          # 25 values
RESTORE_LABELS = (0x11, 0x12, 0x13, 0x17, 0x1B)                        # SPSR-bearing
MSR_LABELS = (0x11, 0x12, 0x13, 0x17, 0x1B, 0x1F)                      # USR strips the c-byte
WORDS["msr_spsr_fsxc_r1"] = 0xE16FF001       # msr spsr_fsxc, r1
WORDS["movs_pc_r2"] = 0xE1B0F002             # movs pc, r2
WORDS["ldm_r6_pc"] = 0xE8968000              # ldmia r6, {pc}        (non-S, PC)
WORDS["ldm_r6_r0_s"] = 0xE8D60001            # ldmia r6, {r0}^       (S, no PC)
WORDS["ldm_r6_r0"] = 0xE8960001              # ldmia r6, {r0}        (non-S, no PC)
GRID_CHECK = {"msr_spsr_fsxc_r1": lambda w: w & 0x0FFFFFF0 == 0x016FF000 and w & 0xF == 1,
              "movs_pc_r2": lambda w: (w >> 21) & 0xF == 0xD and (w >> 20) & 1
              and (w >> 12) & 0xF == 15 and w & 0xFFF == 2 and (w >> 25) & 7 == 0,
              "ldm_r6_pc": lambda w: w & 0x0FFFFFFF == 0x08968000,
              "ldm_r6_r0_s": lambda w: w & 0x0FFFFFFF == 0x08D60001,
              "ldm_r6_r0": lambda w: w & 0x0FFFFFFF == 0x08960001}
GBAD = [k for k, f in GRID_CHECK.items() if not f(WORDS[k]) or WORDS[k] >> 28 != 0xE]
for k in ("ldm_r6_pc_s",):
    if WORDS[k] & 0x0FFFFFFF != 0x08D68000:
        GBAD.append(k)

HI1, HI0 = 0xFFFFFFE0, 0xFFFFFFC0             # bits 31:5 set (T set) / bits 31:6 set (T clear)


def grid_obs(path):
    """[(kind, label, OLD, NEW, WANT)] for one path -- the whole generated table."""
    labels = MSR_LABELS if path == "msr" else RESTORE_LABELS
    obs = []

    def add(kind, lab, new, pol):
        old = lab if pol == 1 else (HI0 | lab)
        newv = (HI1 | new) if pol == 1 else new
        if kind == "acc":
            want = newv
        elif kind == "dec":
            want = (newv & ~0x1F) | lab
        else:                                    # non-restoring pop forms
            want = old
        obs.append((kind, lab, old, newv, want))

    i = 0
    for lab in labels:                           # G2 complement declines, both polarities
        for pol in (1, 2):
            add("dec", lab, lab ^ 0x1F, pol)
    for m in UNMODELLED:                         # G3 every unmodelled value
        add("dec", labels[i % len(labels)], m, 1 + (i % 2))
        i += 1
    j = 0
    for lab in labels:                           # accepts: every modelled mode, both polarities,
        for pol in (1, 2):                       # mode != label
            cands = [m for m in MODELLED if m != lab]
            add("acc", lab, cands[(j * 3) % len(cands)], pol)
            j += 1
    for m in MODELLED:                           # and every modelled mode as an accept target
        lab = [l for l in labels if l != m][j % (len(labels) - 1)]
        add("acc", lab, m, 1 + (j % 2))
        j += 1
    if path == "pop":                            # G4 the three non-restoring enclosing cells
        for kind in ("ldm_pc", "ldm_r0_s", "ldm_r0"):
    #  *** 204 -> 226, 2026-10-02, WITH #529's COMMIT.  Repair 15 (the TIME and REV decline carriers,
    #  the entries 13-and-17 pair) and repair 16 (the CONTROL arm's residue-text count) took the
    #  probe to 226 rows; the chain closed on repair 16's F2 pass. ***  MEASURED, and the logs are
    #  named: table16_20260926/ ran 25 legs on the 226-row instrument, its base 226/226; and the
    #  base leg of the COMMITTED bytes -- repair 16's code with the F2 ruling's text, code-identical
    #  by AST, with the r17 audit's and the ACCEPTS enumeration's docstring corrections -- is
    #  final529r22_20261002/probe_base_r22.raw, 226/226, run BEFORE this pin moved.
    check "crime_width rows run"     "${cres#*/}" 226
    #  *** AND TWO SIBLING PINS CARRIED THE SAME STALE 188.  I fixed the denominator first and
    #  only saw these by reading the next two lines -- which is this project's own rule:
    #  when correcting a claim, GREP FOR ITS SIBLINGS before calling it fixed.  The flagship
    #  seat had said "reds three checks"; one pin would have left two of the three red. ***
    check "crime_width rows correct" "${cres%/*}" "226"
    check "crime_width rows emitted" "$(grep -cE ' (ok|FAIL)$' "$CRIMELOG")" "226"
    check "crime_width rows failed"  "$(grep -c ' FAIL$' "$CRIMELOG")" "0"
fi

gate_end
    fi
}

note() { printf "  --   %s\n" "$*"; }

# A gate may declare that part of its coverage did not run (a rig whose image is absent,
# say). That must not be absorbed into a PASS: it downgrades the whole gate to SKIP, so a
# missing input can never be reported as evidence of correctness.
_degraded=0
degrade() { _degraded=1; printf "  ${C_SKIP}skip${C_OFF} %s\n" "$*"; }

gate_end() {
    echo
    if [ "$_checks" = 0 ]; then
        echo "${C_SKIP}$GATE_NAME: SKIP${C_OFF} (no checks ran)"; return $EXIT_SKIP
    elif [ "$_fails" != 0 ]; then
        echo "${C_BAD}$GATE_NAME: FAIL${C_OFF} ($_fails of $_checks checks)"; return 1
    elif [ "$_degraded" != 0 ]; then
        echo "${C_SKIP}$GATE_NAME: SKIP${C_OFF} ($_checks checks passed, but part of the gate could not run)"
        return $EXIT_SKIP
    else
        echo "${C_OK}$GATE_NAME: PASS${C_OFF} ($_checks checks)"; return 0
    fi
}

#  #371: gate_skip() exits with the SKIP code, which run.sh maps to
#  REGRESS_PASS_WITH_GAPS -- a green-ish verdict. It never consulted the running
#  failure counter, so a gate that had ALREADY recorded red checks and then hit a
#  gate_skip (a probe that produced no result line, a missing rig image) reported
#  those reds as a skip and they vanished from the battery. Measured on a
#  synthetic gate: one FAIL then gate_skip exited 77, PASS_WITH_GAPS. This is the
#  same "a green row means nothing" class the gate rows themselves guard against,
#  in the harness's own control flow.

	elif [ "$_spinned" -gt "$SCORE_CLAIMS_MANIFEST" ]; then

		bad "  This is not a failure -- it means a new gated probe arrived.  Raise"

		bad "  Growth is caught on purpose: a floor that only catches shrinkage goes"

Y(ret)


/*
 *  msr: Move to status register from a normal register or immediate value.
 *
 *  arg[0] = immediate value
 *  arg[1] = mask
 *  arg[2] = pointer to rm
 *
 *  msr_imm and msr_imm_spsr use arg[1] and arg[0].
 *  msr and msr_spsr use arg[1] and arg[2].
 */
X(msr_imm)
{
	uint32_t mask = ic->arg[1];

	if ((cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE) == ARM_MODE_USR32) {
		mask &= 0xff000000;
	}

	/*
	 *  #562: a mode-field write naming a mode this model does not bank
	 *  (reserved, or 26-bit) used to reach arm_load_register_bank()'s
	 *  exit(1).  The architecture makes the result UNPREDICTABLE (DDI
	 *  0100I A2.5.7; ARMv7 lets it be UNDEFINED) and forbids halting.
	 *  Declining the MODE FIELD only, with the rest of the write applied,
	 *  is this emulator's POLICY, not an architectural requirement.  Only
	 *  the local mask changes: ic->arg[] is a cached translation.
	 */
	if ((mask & ARM_FLAG_MODE) && !arm_mode_is_modelled(ic->arg[0])) {
		if (!(cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned & ARM_MODEWARN_MSR)) {
			cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned |= ARM_MODEWARN_MSR;
			fatal("[ msr_imm: mode 0x%02x is not modelled; mode field"
			    " not written, other fields are (once) ]\n",
			    (int)(ic->arg[0] & ARM_FLAG_MODE));
		}
		mask &= ~ARM_FLAG_MODE;
	}

	int switch_register_banks = (mask & ARM_FLAG_MODE) &&
	    ((cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE) !=
	    (ic->arg[0] & ARM_FLAG_MODE));
	uint32_t new_value = ic->arg[0];

	cpu->cd.arm.cpsr &= 0x0fffffff;
	cpu->cd.arm.cpsr |= (cpu->cd.arm.flags << 28);

	if (switch_register_banks)
		arm_save_register_bank(cpu);

	cpu->cd.arm.cpsr &= ~mask;
	cpu->cd.arm.cpsr |= (new_value & mask);

	cpu->cd.arm.flags = cpu->cd.arm.cpsr >> 28;

	if (switch_register_banks)
		arm_load_register_bank(cpu);
}
Y(msr_imm)
X(msr)
{
	ic->arg[0] = reg(ic->arg[2]);
	instr(msr_imm)(cpu, ic);
}
Y(msr)
X(msr_imm_spsr)
{
	uint32_t mask = ic->arg[1];
	uint32_t new_value = ic->arg[0];
	switch (cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE) {
	case ARM_MODE_FIQ32:
		cpu->cd.arm.spsr_fiq &= ~mask;
		cpu->cd.arm.spsr_fiq |= (new_value & mask);
		break;
	case ARM_MODE_ABT32:
		cpu->cd.arm.spsr_abt &= ~mask;
		cpu->cd.arm.spsr_abt |= (new_value & mask);
		break;
	case ARM_MODE_UND32:
		cpu->cd.arm.spsr_und &= ~mask;
		cpu->cd.arm.spsr_und |= (new_value & mask);
		break;
	case ARM_MODE_IRQ32:
		cpu->cd.arm.spsr_irq &= ~mask;
		cpu->cd.arm.spsr_irq |= (new_value & mask);
		break;
	if (exit_debugger)
		cmd[0] = '\0';

	return cmd;
}


/*
 *  debugger_stop_step_call():
 *
 *  Ends "step call" mode: restores the show_trace_tree setting and clears
 *  the per-cpu function call/return transition flags.
 */
static void debugger_stop_step_call(void)
{
	int i;

	if (!debugger_stepping_until_function_transition)
		return;

	debugger_stepping_until_function_transition = 0;
	debugger_machine->show_trace_tree = debugger_old_show_trace_tree;

	for (i=0; i<debugger_machine->ncpus; i++)
		debugger_machine->cpus[i]->last_was_function_transition = 0;
}


/*
 *  debugger():
 *
 *  This is a loop, which reads a command from the terminal, and executes it.
 */
void debugger(void)
{
	int i, cmd_len;
	char *cmd;

	/*  "step call": continue single-stepping, without interaction,
	    until a function call or return has been traced (or the user
	    has pressed CTRL-C):  */
	if (debugger_stepping_until_function_transition) {
		int transition = ctrl_c != 0;

		for (i=0; i<debugger_machine->ncpus; i++)
			if (debugger_machine->cpus[i]->
			    last_was_function_transition)
				transition = 1;

		if (!transition)
			return;

		debugger_stop_step_call();
	}

	if (debugger_n_steps_left_before_interaction > 0) {
		debugger_n_steps_left_before_interaction --;
		return;
	}

	/*
	 *  Clear all dyntrans translations, because otherwise things would
	 *  become to complex to keep in sync.
	 */
	/*  TODO: In all machines  */
	for (i=0; i<debugger_machine->ncpus; i++)
		if (debugger_machine->cpus[i]->translation_cache != NULL) {
			cpu_create_or_reset_tc(debugger_machine->cpus[i]);
			debugger_machine->cpus[i]->
			    invalidate_translation_caches(
			    debugger_machine->cpus[i], 0, INVALIDATE_ALL);
		}

	/*  Stop timers while interacting with the user:  */
	timer_stop();

	exit_debugger = false;
	exit_debugger_to_continue_single_stepping = false;

	while (!exit_debugger) {
		/*  Read a line from the terminal:  */
		cmd = debugger_readline();

		cmd_len = strlen(cmd);

		/*  Remove spaces:  */
		while (cmd_len > 0 && cmd[0]==' ')
			memmove(cmd, cmd+1, cmd_len --);
		while (cmd_len > 0 && cmd[cmd_len-1] == ' ')
			cmd[(cmd_len--)-1] = '\0';

        print("MUTANT-TEXT-NOT-FOUND %s %s count=%d" % (M, f, n))
        sys.exit(3)
    io.open(p, "w", newline="").write(s.replace(a, b))
print("MUTANT-APPLIED %s" % M)
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""mutr4.py <tree> <mutant>|LIST|LANE1|LANE2|LANE3 -- Opus pass-2R4 candidates for #562 repair 4.
Find-text must occur EXACTLY ONCE (presence pin), else exit 3."""
import io
import sys

T, M = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
C = "src/cpus/cpu_arm.c"
I = "src/cpus/cpu_arm_instr.c"
D = "src/cpus/cpu_arm_instr_dpi.c"

PROJ = "\tswitch (mode & ARM_FLAG_MODE) {"
MSR_DEC = "\t\tmask &= ~ARM_FLAG_MODE;\n"
MSR_LATCH = "\t\tif (!(cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned & ARM_MODEWARN_MSR)) {\n"
POP_LATCH = "\t\t\tif (!(cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned & ARM_MODEWARN_POP)) {\n"
DPI_LATCH = "\t\t\tif (!(cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned & ARM_MODEWARN_DPI)) {\n"
POP_GUARD = "\t\tif (!arm_mode_is_modelled(new_cpsr)) {"
CUR = "(cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE)"
NL, TB = chr(10), chr(9)
POP_TAIL = TB*3 + "    (cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE);" + NL + TB*2 + "}" + NL + NL + TB*2 + "switch_register_banks"

MUT = {
    # (A) helper projection NARROWED by bit 3 -- maps 0x18-0x1a onto 0x10-0x12; no driven value moves
    "H_narrow_17": [(C, PROJ, "\tswitch (mode & 0x17) {")],
    # control: narrowed by bit 2 -- 0x14 -> 0x10, predicted KILLED (kill 14)
    "H_narrow_1b": [(C, PROJ, "\tswitch (mode & 0x1b) {")],
    # (A') helper admits 0x18 (the 'admitting' class at an undriven value)
    "H_case_18": [(C, "\tcase ARM_MODE_UND32:\n\t\treturn 1;", "\tcase ARM_MODE_UND32:\n\tcase 0x18:\n\t\treturn 1;")],
    # (B) msr decline width x label: the contract says "fewer than all five mode bits, at any label"
    "M_msr_clr0f_iaus": [(I, MSR_DEC, "\t\tmask &= (" + CUR + " == ARM_MODE_SVC32 || " + CUR +
                          " == ARM_MODE_FIQ32) ? ~ARM_FLAG_MODE : ~0x0f;\n")],
    "M_msr_clr0f_irq": [(I, MSR_DEC, "\t\tmask &= (" + CUR + " == ARM_MODE_IRQ32) ? ~0x0f : ~ARM_FLAG_MODE;\n")],
    "M_msr_clr03_abt": [(I, MSR_DEC, "\t\tmask &= (" + CUR + " == ARM_MODE_ABT32) ? ~0x03 : ~ARM_FLAG_MODE;\n")],
    "M_msr_clr17_fiq": [(I, MSR_DEC, "\t\tmask &= (" + CUR + " == ARM_MODE_FIQ32) ? ~0x17 : ~ARM_FLAG_MODE;\n")],
    # (C) closure rule (2) at the LATCH-SET decline for bit 5 (T): T from the old CPSR once latched
    "N_pop_Tlatch": [(I, POP_LATCH, "\t\t\tif (cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned & ARM_MODEWARN_POP)\n"
                      "\t\t\t\tnew_cpsr = (new_cpsr & ~ARM_FLAG_T) | (cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_T);\n" + POP_LATCH)],
    "N_dpi_Tlatch": [(D, DPI_LATCH, "\t\t\tif (cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned & ARM_MODEWARN_DPI)\n"
                      "\t\t\t\tnew_cpsr = (new_cpsr & ~ARM_FLAG_T) | (cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_T);\n" + DPI_LATCH)],
    "N_msr_Tlatch": [(I, MSR_LATCH, "\t\tif (cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned & ARM_MODEWARN_MSR)\n"
                      "\t\t\tmask &= ~ARM_FLAG_T;\n" + MSR_LATCH)],
    # control: same shape on NZCV at the DPI latch-set decline -- predicted KILLED at R3
    "N_dpi_flagslatch": [(D, DPI_LATCH, "\t\t\tif (cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned & ARM_MODEWARN_DPI)\n"
                          "\t\t\t\tnew_cpsr = (new_cpsr & 0x0fffffff) | (cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & 0xf0000000);\n" + DPI_LATCH)],
    # (C') rule (1) at the latch-set ACCEPT for bit 5: a T-set valid SPSR declined once latched
    "E_pop_Tacc": [(I, POP_GUARD, "\t\tif (!arm_mode_is_modelled(new_cpsr) ||\n"
                    "\t\t    ((cpu->cd.arm.mode_warned & ARM_MODEWARN_POP) && (new_cpsr & ARM_FLAG_T))) {")],
    # (D) disclosed: the decline writes the cached translation's mask
    "M_msr_argmask": [(I, MSR_DEC, "\t\tic->arg[1] &= ~ARM_FLAG_MODE;\n" + MSR_DEC)],
    # (E) closure rule (2) polarity: a decline FORCING a bit 31:8 to 1 (contract: "or forcing it")
    "N_dpi_forceN": [(D, "new_cpsr = (new_cpsr & ~ARM_FLAG_MODE) | cur_mode;",
                      "new_cpsr = (new_cpsr & ~ARM_FLAG_MODE) | cur_mode | 0x80000000;")],
    "N_pop_forceN": [(I, POP_TAIL, POP_TAIL.replace("ARM_FLAG_MODE);", "ARM_FLAG_MODE) | 0x80000000;", 1))],
    # (E') the cell (SPSR bit 0, old bit 1): a decline that ORs in the old CPSR's bits 31:8
    "N_pop_orold": [(I, POP_TAIL, POP_TAIL.replace("ARM_FLAG_MODE);", "ARM_FLAG_MODE) | (cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & 0xffffff00);", 1))],
    "N_dpi_orold": [(D, "new_cpsr = (new_cpsr & ~ARM_FLAG_MODE) | cur_mode;",
                     "new_cpsr = (new_cpsr & ~ARM_FLAG_MODE) | cur_mode | (cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & 0xffffff00);")],
    # control: msr decline forcing N -- predicted KILLED (msr wide: operand N = 0)
    "M_msr_forceN": [(I, MSR_DEC, TB + TB + "mask &= ~(ARM_FLAG_MODE | 0x80000000);" + NL + TB + TB + "cpu->cd.arm.flags |= 8;" + NL)],
}
LANES = {
    "LANE1": ["H_narrow_17", "H_narrow_1b", "H_case_18", "M_argmask_placeholder"],
    "LANE2": ["M_msr_clr0f_iaus", "M_msr_clr0f_irq", "M_msr_clr03_abt", "M_msr_clr17_fiq"],
    "LANE4": ["N_dpi_forceN", "N_pop_forceN", "N_pop_orold", "N_dpi_orold", "M_msr_forceN"],
    "LANE3": ["N_pop_Tlatch", "N_dpi_Tlatch", "N_msr_Tlatch", "N_dpi_flagslatch", "E_pop_Tacc"],
}
LANES["LANE1"][-1] = "M_msr_argmask"
if M == "LIST":
    print(" ".join(MUT))
    sys.exit(0)
if M in LANES:
    print(" ".join(LANES[M]))
    sys.exit(0)
for f, a, b in MUT[M]:
    p = T + "/" + f
    s = io.open(p, newline="").read()
    n = s.count(a)
    if n != 1:
        print("MUTANT-TEXT-NOT-FOUND %s %s count=%d" % (M, f, n))
        sys.exit(3)
    io.open(p, "w", newline="").write(s.replace(a, b))
print("MUTANT-APPLIED %s" % M)
> adopted: it changes what every probe in the tree must carry, and that is an owner decision
> rather than a round's.*
>
> **The third is the one that looked impossible and is the cheapest.** It only seemed
> unreachable because it was approached from the mutant side -- *"what edit escapes?"*,
> which is unbounded -- instead of the field side -- *"what states can these two fields be
> in?"*, which is a grep. In `#462` that was three lines of output: `alloc_data` sets both
> fields; two `free` sites clear only one.
>
> **DO NOT move the ask-again earlier.** It works BECAUSE a clean table redirects seats away
> from shapes the author already covered; measured yield says so. And do not raise the cap
> -- the cap is what stands between this and `#392`.

> **(e) THE CONTRACT.** The probe header names which edits this detector is designed
> to catch. *A detector that says so is falsifiable; one claiming completeness is
> not* -- and a later escape becomes "outside the contract" or "a breach of it",
> which are different dispositions, rather than an argument about overclaiming.
> Two lines in a header.
>
> **IT IS A FLOOR CLAUSE, so a breach is L2 �?" replace IN-ROUND however late it is found.**
> That is deliberate and it is the whole point: an escaped (d) mutant means the round's
> claim to have "shipped a detector with the fix" is false about the fix's own repair.
>
> ### *** (f) THE ANCHOR RULE -- adopted 2026-08-25 after a CLEAN 12/12 detector was escaped
> ### by a ONE-TERM translation that three purpose-built rows could not see BY CONSTRUCTION. ***
>
> **Rows that assert only PAIRWISE DISTINCTNESS, or a same-path round-trip, test the decode
> against ITSELF.** They are therefore invariant under any edit that re-maps addresses
> INJECTIVELY over the probed set -- translations first among them -- and they catch a re-map
> only where it COLLIDES on that set.
names no probe, and guessing would produce false positives, which is how a check
gets ignored.  Those still depend on a reading seat.  This closes the half where
the pin and the prose sit in the same file, which is where three of the four
incidents were.
"""
import io
import os
import re
import sys

#  This file lives in tools/pipeline/, so the repo root is TWO levels up from
#  the directory it sits in -- three dirnames, not two.  The first draft used
#  two and resolved regress/ to tools/regress/, which does not exist.
ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))
REGRESS = os.path.join(ROOT, "regress")

#  check "<stem> rows run"     "${x#*/}" N
PIN = re.compile(r'check\s+"(\S+)\s+rows\s+run"\s+"[^"]*"\s+(\d+)')
#  *** A BARE d+/d+ MATCHES A DATE, AND A PANEL SEAT MEASURED IT. ***  On
#  "measured 2026/08/23" the old pattern returned (2026, 08) and would have
#  compared 08 against a row count.  The flanking guards reject anything that
#  is part of a longer slash-run (a date, a path, a/b/c) or a percentage.
#  *** THE `x` IN THE LOOKBEHIND IS LOAD-BEARING AND WAS ADDED 2026-08-26 (`scoreclaimhex`).
#  Without it this reads a SLASH-SEPARATED HEX LIST as a score claim: `bases
#  0x00/0x10/0x20/0x30` -- one line of ordinary prose in asic_badaddr_probe.py's
#  docstring -- yielded THREE stale-claim FAILs (`00/0`, `10/0`, `20/0`) against a pin of
#  12, and NONE of them had ever been written. The `00` is preceded by `x`, which the old
#  `(?<![\d/])` happily allowed.
#
#  A FALSE FAIL IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE FAILURE THIS SECTION EXISTS TO CATCH, AND IS STILL

VERDICT: DO-NOT-SHIP | 1 floor breach

I count **one contract-floor class: first-decline restore corruption while the warning latch is clear**. The cached-mask escape is disclosed debt under the local rule’s explicit bound. Gate integration is also unfinished. No additional product-code defect established.

Read-only review only: no writes, builds, detector runs, or gates.

**1 | The aggregate grid passes; the promised latch-conditioned coverage does not.**

I independently checked the generator and the handwritten observations:

| Static denominator | Coverage |
|---|---:|
| Generated observations | MSR 56; DPI 52; POP 58 |
| G1: path × outcome × bit × polarity | 369/369 after 15 named exclusions |
| G2: label × five mode bits | 80/80 |
| G3: 25 declined + 7 accepted modes, each path | 96/96 |
| G4: POP enclosing cells × two polarities | 8/8 |
| Unconditioned force-zero/force-one shapes | 378/384; six equivalent bit-4-force-one cases |
| Unconditioned non-mode keep-old shapes | 162/162 |

These are **static coverage calculations, not additional measured mutant kills**.

The breach appears when latch state is retained. For **first-clear decline × bits 31:8 × three paths**, the denominator is **72**. `msr wide` distinguishes MSR’s 24 cells; **DPI and POP leave 48 unobserved**:

- Their first full-word grid declines expect all those bits already set.
- Their first `src` declines also expect those bits set.
- Other first-decline observations inspect mode, I/F, or the low byte.
- Subsequent restores or debugger resets overwrite the upper bits before another full-word observation.

A small candidate is one additional assignment forcing N in `new_cpsr` inside either restore’s existing latch-clear announcement block. **Static analysis predicts survival; I have not measured 47/47 for it.** The owed measurements are the first-clear-only counterparts of `N_dpi_forceN` and `N_pop_forceN`.

This directly contradicts the [contract’s force-one claim at both latch states](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/arm_msrmode_probe.py:114). The [local contract rule](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/CLAUDE.md:1714) distinguishes such breaches from disclosed exclusions. That is the single class counted above.

A wider static check also finds **126/160** distinguishing label × latch × mode-bit cells: MSR 40/60, DPI 43/50, POP 43/50. Missing cells are:

- MSR: CLEAR × IRQ/ABT/UND/SYS × all five bits.
- Each restore: CLEAR IRQ/bit 1; ABT/bits 0,1,2; UND/bits 0,1,3.

G2’s aggregate claim remains true; it does not establish this larger product.

**2 | `M_msr_argmask`: disclosure is defensible under the adopted bound.**

The supplied extra cached-mask write is a **measured small edit preserving 47/47**. I verified its preserved log; I do not claim global syntactic minimality.

The source comment establishes a real correctness invariant. Nevertheless, [DEPENDS-ON’s explicit enumeration](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/CLAUDE.md:1393) covers dominating predicates and specified guard-operand swaps. This mutant retains the current decline’s repair and adds a persistent side effect. Translation reuse is not an existing body-tested partition. The comment alone does not expand that bounded enumeration.

Therefore I would **not require the loop as a floor row under that rule**. A register-form reuse observation remains valuable disclosed coverage. Repeating an immediate instruction would not necessarily distinguish this particular mutation, because its operand remains unchanged.

**3 | Several records still need correction.**

- **G1’s source statement is false for declined mode bits.** Those result bits equal OLD, not NEW. [`grid_cov()` correctly uses NEW as the wrong-source alternative for those bits](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/arm_msrmode_probe.py:1136); the header should match.
- **`grid cov` certifies only aggregate G1.** It does not assert G2 label coverage, G3 mode coverage, G4 enclosing cells, or latch-conditioned coverage. Those currently hold only to the extents independently counted above.
- **The debugger correction is substantively right but its “only” is false.** [`debugger()` also returns before clearing during `step call`](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/debugger/debugger.c:764). The clearing loop covers the selected machine’s CPUs. Neither qualification rescues this probe’s cached-translation coverage.
- **PRETABLE5 still contains the withdrawn register-form assertion.** Preserve its preregistration history and append a dated correction identifying the failed prediction and measured escape.
- **The latch-clear force-one contract overclaims**, as explained above.

The central measurements check out: byte-identical fix4/fix5 diffs; identical probe ASTs excluding the module docstring; 110 mutant logs covering 104 names; only `M_msr_argmask` green; all 16 lane baselines 47/47; fixed 47/47 and HEAD 18/47 with exactly the stated 29 reds and 43 sessions.

**4 | The proposed gate scores the preserved logs correctly, but it is not installed.**

The actual [gate_arm.sh](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_arm.sh:1946) has no `arm_msrmode` section and ends with `gate_end`.

Before shipping:

- Insert the section **before `gate_end`**. That function returns a status; appending checks afterward can overwrite the failure status and print the verdict prematurely.
- Raise [the score-pin manifest](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/precommit_check.sh:346) from **32 to 33**. The proposed unquoted `47` and single-token stem correctly match the checker.
- Replace the `<OWED ...>` measurement placeholder with the required named final-probe receipt.

The snippet’s patterns correctly recount fixed **47 emitted / 0 FAIL** and HEAD **47 emitted / 29 FAIL**. It checks unique completion records, scores, exit status, and `REDS=none`.

Its remaining limitation is aggregate trust: it does not reconcile row identities/statuses against the result records. Duplicated or contradictory row-shaped output could pass with favorable aggregates. The current producer derives these consistently, so this is a parser limitation, **not an established product-mutant escape**. Also, legitimate `CONTROL=DEAD` output omits REDS and is consequently reported as malformed; it still fails closed.
